Godscar defense experiment: stage-based armor K and monster crit calibration
SIM_VERSION 7; candidate k=0.00045, monster HP=1x. Twelve sim-hour fixed-stage replays; Rare Paladin optimizer snapshots; seeds 31 and 2026. No production simulation changes.
K now uses the monster’s average unsplit hit at that stage. The comparison grid replays the previous N=1 crit settings under the old per-hit reference and stage-average reference at stages 62/70; because N=1 hit equals the unsplit hit, results should match exactly.
The test-only crit-reduction value reduces monster crit chance proportionally: effective chance = base chance × (1 − reduction). It does not reduce crit multiplier. A zero base chance short-circuits before RNG, so crit rolls consume no random numbers at 0%.
Calibration grid contains every chance (3/5/8%), multiplier (1.5/2/2.5x), reduction (0/25/50%), seed, stage (62/70/80), and armor multiplier (0.75/1/1.5). Deaths/hour = observed deaths divided by 12 sim hours.
Requested tiers use underprepared 0.75x armor/reduction 0%; prepared 1x/reduction 0%; well prepared 1.5x/reduction 50%. Summary averages cover both seeds and all three stages; raw lines preserve each run.

N=1 K-REFERENCE EQUALITY CHECKS:

Existing-stat check: no current stat reduces monster critical chance or damage. Dodge can avoid an attack; it is the closest existing percentage-based defense. Crit reduction would be a new mechanic, though it could reuse the existing percentage-stat and affix plumbing. It must remain separate from dodge because dodge avoids the whole hit.
Working candidate remains k=0.00045 and 1x monster HP; it moves the survival cliff deeper but does not remove it.
Unverified: only two seeds, Rare Paladin snapshots and Colossus stages 62/70/80. No other classes, gear fixtures, stages, or player-controlled builds tested.
